## Tracker: the r63 audit and what it placed (r64 and r65; CLAUDE.md, How work is done 10)

The one list of what the r63 audit found and of the open items it placed, so nothing is dropped between sessions. Sources: the audit's ledger (`claude/ironlog-r63-audit-ledger.md` in V's Working Out project), the plan doc "Ironlog r63 Audit and Hardening Plan" (claude.ai artifact 4aQuNCbm5jrcjuZmSChfhc) and V's answers of 2026-10-27 (decisions 1, 2, 3, 5 and 9 as recommended; 8: 339 n deferred, not its own release; 4: no ticket fixer was set up, checked below; 6: this session has push access; 7: 325 a done, 341 a on V's phone). Statuses: OPEN (found and verified), FIXED (commit and a test that fails before it), NOT REPRODUCED, NOT A PROBLEM, DECISION PENDING, DEFERRED, NEW. A row is never deleted or merged; IDs are the ledger's (P- personas, R- rules, A- architecture, F- real-life conditions) or PENDING's numbers; the full review of 2026-11-17's rows (L01-, L2-, L3-, L4- and P-) are its ledger's, `claude/ironlog-full-review-ledger-2026-11-17.md`, whose P- rows are the page's own files. Evidence names the audit's reproduction (its scripts are archived in the project as `claude/ironlog-r63-audit-scripts.b64.txt`) or what this session checked itself.

One rule, in one place (toast.ts; CLAUDE.md, Messages): what one tap says is joined into one message, never one over another (a tap read by tasks and the lifter's next touch, click or key, not by time); news from storage and sync (marked in main.ts, the one place it reaches the page) waits while a tap's message shows and comes back if a tap covers it before it is read; an ask lives where it applies, so the bodyweight ask is a line on Plan under the day's first bodyweight lift, not a message; exercise creation says "created" beside what the pick said. | NEW-124 | Found 2026-11-11 by Claude writing the saving and sync checks (S-6): two tabs, the same record changed in both, the tab whose change meets the other tab's save not yet heard keeps the other tab's earlier edit, drops its own later one, and says "1 item was edited on two devices. The later edit was kept." | 2.53 | FIXED | Reproduced on 2.49 (f8e47d1): tests/storage-sync.js S-6: tab A sets a session's notes to "from A", then tab B, not yet having heard A's save, sets them to "from B"; the save holds "from A" and B says the later edit was kept. The cause: sync.ts mergeIn merges with the other tab's copy winning every clash (`remoteWins` true), including when the merge is this tab's own save (`own`), whose change is the newest. How often a lifter meets it: only when a tab changes a record before it has heard the other tab's save of the same record. Fixed in d744c64: on this tab's own save the change just made wins a clash, on hearing the other tab's save that save does (sync.ts mergeIn, `!own`). tests/storage-sync.js S-6 fails on baselines/r95.html (2.49) and passes; tests/integrity.js (two tabs), sync-race.js, dataflow.js and messages.js pass. Reopened 2026-11-11 (CLAUDE.md, How work is done 10): the independent review of 2.50 and 2.51 found the fix lost what the other tab logged (REV12-1 to REV12-4), so it is withdrawn in 2.50 before any merge and mergeIn keeps 2.49's rule (the other tab's copy, saved first, wins). S-6 now checks that rule exactly ("keeps one whole copy, the one saved first (NEW-124 OPEN)"), and the review's four cases. A fix that keeps the later edit needs each tab's time of change, a workout's sets kept whichever copy wins (as a sync merge keeps them, keepLost), and Undo's step read after the merge; planned with REV12-6 for the release after 2.52. 2.53 (883b6c2, V's go 2026-11-11 18:56, "keep going until all of these are appropriately resolved", on Claude's recommendation of 18:40): a tab shown again takes in what the other tab saved meanwhile before anything else (sync.ts onVisibility), so a change made there next is the later one and is kept; tests/storage-sync.js S-6 "a tab shown again catches up" and "so the change made there after is the later one" fail on 2.52's build (baselines/r98.html: "first", then tab A's note kept with "The later edit was kept") and pass. Left as 2.49 has it, by design: two tabs both on screen changing one item in the moment before either hears the other keep the other tab's copy, and the words now say so, "1 item was changed in two tabs at once. The other tab's change was kept." (conflictNote; S-6's words fail on r98 and pass). Keeping the later of the two there needs each change's time and items merged field by field, between devices too, for a clash a lifter meets only with two tabs side by side: not built (CLAUDE.md 7) |

| NEW-144 | Found 2026-11-12 23:30 by GitHub's WebKit run of 2.63 (run 43380005789, both builds): tests/week.js section 4, "a tap on the bar while it is open closes it", the calendar stayed open (Chromium: it closes). The cause is not known yet. Either Playwright's WebKit sends a finger's tap on as a click only to an element that responds to clicks itself, as an iPhone does, and the layer around a sheet is a plain element (the app listens for taps on the whole page), or the tap reached the bar under the layer. If the first, a finger on the dimmed area outside any sheet may not close it on an iPhone either, which no suite covers (they tap outside with a mouse click) | 2.63 | FIXED | The next run's notes (run 43387923858, WebKit, both builds) settle the cause: the finger reached the layer (`{"at":"#modal","touch":"#modal","click":null}`), no click followed, and a finger on the dimmed area above History's Calendar, an ordinary sheet, left it open too; in Chromium both closed. So in WebKit, and very likely on an iPhone, which taps the same way, a finger on the dimmed area outside any sheet closes nothing, in the live app as well; the mouse clicks of every suite hid it. Fixed in the app in 2.63 (commit below): the tap outside a sheet is heard on the layer itself (events.ts `onOutside`), so the layer listens for clicks and a finger's tap there becomes one. Checks: week.js section 4's tap on the bar (failing in WebKit before, run 43387923858) and gym-ux's finger on the dimmed area above the picker; both pass in Chromium before and after, so the fix is shown by GitHub's WebKit runs: failing before (run 43387923858), passing with 755a657 (run 43396913314, every job green), its notes reading {"at":"#modal","touch":"#modal","click":"#modal"} and the calendar closed, and History's Calendar closed by a finger above it. Found with it: gym-ux's check of a half-filled new exercise kept after an outside tap clicked at 12 px, the tall sheet's own edge, never outside it; it now clicks above the sheet, and passes. To try on V's phone: open any sheet and tap the dimmed area above it |

A first install's update prompt in WebKit once out of seven runs: told to V, and to watch for in later runs Then again on 2.60 (2026-11-13 15:00, pull request #105, run's job 132164828204, webkit (2)): the same check, the bar shown on a first install ({"navs":1,"ver":"2026.11.12-r106","bar":true}); its rerun passed. Two of about 25 WebKit runs: intermittent, not a one-off, so OPEN (V told). Older than the stack (the boot script in scripts/build.js is unchanged by it). What a lifter could see: an "Update ready" bar the first time the app is installed, Update then reloading it; nothing is lost. Likely cause, not shown here (WebKit cannot be installed here): on a first install WebKit can report the new worker as reg.waiting and as the page's controller at once, so offer() reads it as an update. Candidate fix: offer only when reg.waiting !== navigator.serviceWorker.controller, which keeps an update in the same visit (r64's F4); it changes the boot script and so the Content Security Policy's hash. Unsaid. A tab sees the other's write only once its own task ends, so storedElsewhere cannot guard a write at the end of a long task and writeTakenIn does not ask it; the tab whose save was replaced takes the other's whole. Window: the taking-in itself, 40 to 70 ms on a 151 KB log, 738 ms with the CPU slowed 6 times. A target, so the lifter can type over it; no stored number is touched. By the numbers, two roundings: whole steps are counted down from the converted load (61.2 kg to 51.2), then the result is put on the unit's loads downward (50). Dumbbells in kg step by 2 kg while their targets sit on the unit's 2.5 kg loads (30 lb read in kg is 12.5 kg; plus from 13.6 kg is 14), so which loads a lift has by default in the other unit is to be written as one rule first, built from the code and what a lifter expects, with a sweep against 2.68 listing every target that moves before any fix. The acceptance review of 2.68's note for the fix (A-6, not a defect): engine.ts `suggestIn` builds the injury, return and lighter-day results in three separate blocks, each passed through `snapUnitIn`, and the two roundings are `lighterLoadIn`, then `snapUnitIn`. Fixed in 6f58e3e: from a load typed in the other unit, a Lighter day, a deload and the way back put the rule's load on the lift's own spacing in its unit once, the heaviest at or under it (loadsteps.ts unitFloorIn in lighterLoadIn); a load in hand keeps its nearest-load count. The rule written first: the loads a lift has in its unit are its spacing (loadGridIn) from the bar or 0, never under one step on a loaded lift. Sweep (claude/ironlog-2.69-sweeps.md in V's project, 1,756 targets: six kinds of lift, both directions, four days, 2.68 against this): 717 move, all Lighter day, deload or return; none on a normal day, none above 90%, every one on the unit's spacing; 27 lower than 2.68's, each 2.68's off the unit's loads (87.52 kg to 87.5); the row's examples now 55 kg, 5 kg, 15 kg, 17.5 kg. Left as it was: kg dumbbells step by 2 kg while their loads sit on 2.5 kg. Test: progression P-45, failing on baselines/r114.html and passing; session L-39's guard restated to the rule (75 kg; 72.5 on 2.68) |

| UAT-4 | The acceptance review of 2.68 (claude/ironlog-2.68-uat-review.md): a storage ceiling on iPhones. The whole log is one string under `ironlog.v1` in localStorage: an 8-set workout saved is 1,242 characters, the log before any workout about 45,000, the exercise list 42,883 of it. WebKit, the engine of every iPhone browser and of the home-screen app, keeps localStorage to 5 MB stored as UTF-16, about 2.5 million characters. Estimate: about 4 to 5 years at four 18-set workouts a week, about 3 at five heavier ones, sooner after importing years of history. At the ceiling: UAT-7, and the only way to make room is deleting sessions | later (the guard in 2.71) | DEFERRED | V, 2026-11-16 11:15, "GO with your recommended": the move of the log to the phone's database deferred (about 3 to 5 years of training at V's volume before the ceiling; the move rewrites how every save is stored and read back), and the guard built: Settings says when the log passes 60% of the limit, with what to do (279534f; storage-sync S-1 (UAT-4), failing on 801a95e and passing). Before: Measured in Chromium on 2.68 by the review; not tested on an iPhone. The ceiling's source, opened here: WebKit bug 245479 ("REGRESSION (iOS 16): LocalStorage is cleared after quota error", resolved fixed), where WebKit says strings are kept as UTF-16, so 5 MB holds about 2.5 million characters. The review's notes with it: the exercise list lives inside every log, the phone's copy and the account's first document (174 lifts, 42,883 characters; that document 44,757 of its 250,000 cap, Recently deleted able to hold 60,000 of it), so 325 b's larger library has room for about 750 lifts before that document nears its cap (A-3); the app shows the log's size nowhere and says nothing as the store fills (A-4); the sync notes keep a fingerprint of each record, not a copy, so an account adds little (A-5). | UAT-13 | The acceptance review of 2.68 (claude/ironlog-2.68-uat-review.md), told V in its first message and left out of its first save: a log kept in a Safari tab, signed out, can be cleared by the phone. Safari deletes a site's stored data after seven days of Safari use with no use of that site; an app added to the home screen is counted apart. The app never asks the phone to keep its storage. Who it reaches: a new friend who tries the site in Safari without installing or signing in, and comes back after a week away | 2.71 in part | OPEN | 2.71 (cec82f6) asks the browser, once a visit at its first save, to keep the site's storage (navigator.storage.persist; storage-sync S-1, failing on baselines/r116.html and passing, in Chromium). Whether it reaches the case found is not shown: WebKit grants the ask by heuristics such as the site opened as a Home Screen web app (webkit.org, Updates to Storage Policy), and WebKit bug 271401 (open) reports persist() answering false in Safari 17. So a signed-out Safari tab may still be cleared after seven days away; the installed app is shielded when the phone runs short of space where it is granted. Not tried on an iPhone. Left for V: a line where it applies (Settings or Today) for a signed-out lifter in a browser tab, saying the log lives only in this browser until the app is added to the home screen or an account is used. Before: Not reproducible here (no iPhone, no Safari). Sources, by the review: WebKit's post of March 2020 on the seven-day cap on script-writable storage, and itnews.com.au on the same (home-screen apps keep their own count). Read here on 6591d97: `navigator.storage.persist` is nowhere in src/, and CLAUDE.md, OWNER.md, ARCHITECTURE.md, README.md and docs/ do not mention the rule. Checked here and not settled: WebKit's storage post of 2023-08-10 says a site in persistent mode may be left out of eviction, and that WebKit grants it by heuristics "like whether the website is opened as a Home Screen Web App"; it does not say whether that lifts the seven-day rule, so whether asking would keep a Safari tab's log is unknown. Today an account keeps a copy, and the home-screen app counts its own days. 2.27-S1 bears on it |

Bears on UAT-1 (the card names no machine), ruled "Leave it as ruled" this morning on Claude's recommendation; named machines would answer it. The proposal sent to V: the sheet lists this exercise's machines by name, newest first, the one in use marked, its last set small beside it; "Add machine" takes a name, offering names used on other exercises; a machine from before names shows its last use until named; the card names the machine in use; the names kept on the exercise, absent until one is named (no change for an old log or another device). Its own release with an independent review, as it changes what is stored. V's question: go as proposed? V, 2026-11-15 14:42: "Yes fix these things too now so go ahead and build it this way. And Streamline other such bullshit like i have said: optimized clean logical experience for users!"; 15:14, on separate releases: "Put it in one release", so it ships in 2.70 (pull request #115), not 2.71. Built in 9028c95 and c2cad8f: the names kept on the exercise (`machines`, absent until one is named, so an old log and its sync hashes are unchanged; normalize.ts machineNames keeps each valid name in one line, 40 characters at most, under a key a machine can have; 2.69 keeps the field when it saves, tests/migration.js); the Machine sheet lists the lift's machines by name with the last session small beside it, or by the session until named, each with ✎ to name it and Add a machine to add one by name (names given on other lifts one tap each, the most used first, only those of machines a lift has: display.ts machineNamesOf); the card names the machine in use, a tap opening the sheet; one Undo step takes a machine added and its name. Tests: session L-41 (machines by name, from the seed to the next week and a name taken off) and personas 9 (Newtech added by the name typed, named on the card the week after), both stopping on r115 at Add a machine, which r115 lacks; migration's names block (r115 saves none and keeps them). Not covered: two tabs naming machines of one lift at once keep the later edit of the exercise, the rule every other change to an exercise follows (the independent review is asked to check it) GitHub's run of 6adeefe: tests/r43.js counted `machines` among an exercise's unknown fields (its list of the fields the app knows predates it), fixed in c1e7fb6 by adding it to that list. | NEW-169 | V, 2026-11-15 14:42: "why does the profile pic(s) look blurry when. Clicked into to enlarge? Why do they not retain their quality or is it just my selected pic?" | 2.70 | FIXED | Reproduced by Claude on r115 (2.69): a picture picked for the profile is kept as a 512 px square (photo.ts PHOTO_EDGE), shown large at 357 pt on a 393 pt iPhone, 1071 screen pixels: stretched about twice, so soft for every picture, not his alone. Fixed in 9028c95: kept at 1024 px, drawn down with the browser's best shrink (pics.ts jpegOf, imageSmoothingQuality high). Test: tests/photo.js section 1 (a 3024 x 4032 camera picture kept as a 1024 px square; shown large on a 393 pt iPhone at no more than about its own size; a striped picture keeps its stripes, a guard), failing on baselines/r115.html ("w":512; "kept":512, "screenPx":1071) and passing. A picture already set stays at 512 until picked again Then GitHub's WebKit run of 6adeefe (job webkit 2) failed the guard: lines shrunk come out whole ({"mean":127,"sd":127.4}), as WebKit's drawImage, every iPhone's browser, kept one pixel of each 3 whatever the smoothing asked; the same numbers here with that drawing's smoothing off. So a photo shrunk on an iPhone was grainy. Fixed in c1e7fb6: the shrink is the app's own area average (pics.ts areaShrink: each kept pixel the average of every pixel it covers), so every browser gives the same; a photo too large to read whole (over 16 million pixels, an iPhone canvas's limit) is drawn smaller first. The guard is now that the lines are averaged: every kept pixel a mix of the lines it covers, none black or white as they are (each 85 or 170 at a 3-times shrink, as an area average gives). Its first form asked for an even grey (sd under 12), a blur past averaging that Chromium's own filter gives and an area average does not; it was this release's, never published. Checked here: the head with smoothing off fails it ({"lo":0,"hi":255}); the fix with smoothing off passes ({"lo":84,"hi":170}). WebKit itself only on GitHub (no WebKit here) |

| REV22-1 | The independent review of 2.70's fixes in c1e7fb6 (claude/ironlog-rev22-review-report.md), new with c1e7fb6: a lift whose first session is marked new (2.69 makes one with a tap), that session deleted: the sessions after it are named on its machine, the next workout saved with none lands on the first machine, so the lift has two machines, a poor day reads as one session alone ("24+ reps at 125 lb, Last (Nov 11)") and a PR is not counted | 2.70 | FIXED | Rerun on 0346640: the same (p01b-first-deleted-target.js: "Today: 24+ reps over 3 sets at 125 lb ... Root fix, chosen by V 2026-11-15 18:50 ("Root fix in 2.70 (Recommended)", asked why machines kept breaking): every session of a machine lift names the machine it was done on (`mc`); a log saved before is named once, as each session reads, when the whole log is read (normalize.ts pinMachines, the index's own rule: sameExOnce, working sets); every session saved names its machine (analytics.ts machineToSaveIn, a new machine by the session's id). So a session moves to another machine only by a pick. keepFollowers and the carry of a lift's mark to a card added later are gone; a card taken out hands its mark to the lift's other cards (worksets.ts keepLiftMachine). Fixed in 16e7d61. Test: session L-41 (REV22-1), failing on 0346640 and passing: one machine, "33+ reps over 3 sets at 125 lb", "Last (Nov 11, below usual)". Built in r60 (V's report, not new scope): on a lift on the full bar typed as the total (`loadFloorIn`, r27's floor for deloads, so the same lifts: a compound barbell lift, or any with a bar of its own set), a set whose load is under the empty bar asks first, as a load far off does, Fix it or Log it, naming the bar where it is set ("0 lb is under the 45 lb bar set in Settings.", "10 kg is under this lift's 15 kg bar."): any set, a warm-up or a drop included, a ticked set changed to it, and Finish's Mark it done; within half the last digit shown of the bar is the bar. A barbell curl or skull crusher has no floor (an EZ or fixed bar is often lighter), nor a load typed per side; and a 0 on a lift with no bar (a dumbbell, a cable) still ticks, since a plate-loaded machine or a landmine can be done with nothing added. `tests/r60.js` section 9 (fails on f4e3a8d and on r59) and persona 6 hold it; the Guide says it under A number far off. 341. V on the live r56.1, 2026-10-24, a chest day after weeks off: (a) editing a load: a grey load (the default since r25, item 71; "Fill in suggested loads" in Settings > Rest timer and logging fills them instead) felt harder to change than a filled number, and one tap to log must stay; to be looked at with the session screen (r60) on the phone, how each kind of row is edited when the load differs from the suggestion, and settled with V before any change (no restyle without V); (b) back after a break, bench eased as it should be, then the first set went well and the next step was only 10 lb, where V expected about 20: the return rule (90%, then normal steps) is 339 (p); V asks that steps adapt to the lift, its kind and the lifter's trend, and that every engine rule be provably right in every scenario, never misleading. Not yet reproduced: reproduce on the live build with V's log before changing anything. Reproduced 2026-10-24 on r56.1 (`baselines/r56-1.html`) with a log built to match (bench 185 x 8, 8, 7 at RIR 1 on 6-10, then six weeks off; V's own log was not available, so which case V hit is not confirmed): (a) tapped, a grey load is an empty field under a hint, so it cannot be edited in place and backspace does nothing, while a filled load takes the caret where tapped and typing inserts (165, then 1, gave 1615); (b) the 10 lb was the next-sets adjustment (bench's next-session step is 5 lb): the return asks 165 for 6-10 with 2+ in reserve, and 165 x 10 at 3 in reserve, or 12 unrated, moves the next sets to 175, with 4 in reserve to 180; the adjustment aims at mid-range at the target RIR plus 1, caps at +10% and rounds down, so it stops short of 185, which that set's estimate (236 lb against 238 before the break) allows at 6 reps with about 3 in reserve; and done as asked (165 x 6 x 3 unrated) the next session asks 165 "beat 18 reps", several sessions of reps back to 185, the app reading its own instruction to stop short as the lifter's limit (339 p). EVIDENCE.md backs the 90% (Bosquet 2013); no source gives a per-session return rate for trained lifters (the retraining studies found are in older or untrained adults), so the fix stays estimate-led and its limits are labelled conventions. Decided by V, 2026-10-24 ("make the decision that is not convoluted and most comprehensive ... record them"): (a) on a tap, a load field, grey or filled, shows its number selected (a digit replaces it, backspace clears it, a second tap places the caret), a grey one left untouched stays grey and follows the next-sets adjustment, one tap to log unchanged, nothing different at rest; built with the session screen (r60), tried on V's iPhone before it ships (iOS selection behaviour), noted to double check; (b) one rule for a load going up: (1) the load is the lift's own estimate's, in whole steps on its rack or plates (the step does not follow the trend line itself, which would chase 2-8% day-to-day noise); (2) a lift's kind keeps its step at the top of the range, one step of about 2.5% (compound) or 5% (isolation), double progression unchanged; (3) the 10% cap applies only past loads the lift has already done in this stretch, so after a break loads up to the pre-break working load are re-approaches bounded by the estimate alone; (4) on the first session back the next sets aim at the bottom of the range with 2 in reserve, the return's own words (165 x 10 at 3 gives 185 x 6, 165 x 6 at 3 gives 170); (5) the session after a return asks the load the return's estimate gives for the usual reps at the target, up to the pre-break load, reading unrated return sets as 2 in reserve, what was asked (165 x 8 x 3 unrated gives 170; r56.2 asks 165, beat 24 reps); this closes 339 (p). Built in r60 beside the next-sets code it changes (src/17-adjust.js moves then), its failing-first test a seeded-history suite across lifts, kinds, units, racks and breaks holding in every case: no return asks past the pre-break load, every adjusted load leaves its stated reps at 2+ in reserve by the same estimate, every load on the rack or plates, an up never lighter than its set, a return done as asked never asking less next time; EVIDENCE.md gets 3 to 5 as conventions. Built in r60 (347 C and D): (a) and (b) as decided; (a) to be tried on V's iPhone before r60 ships. The r63 audit (2026-10-27): (a) did not reproduce in Chromium, still to try on V's iPhone (tracker P-S4-1); (b) the return still stops short of the pre-break load (tracker R-3), fixed in r64 by V's decision 1.

the rest (traps, forearms, front delts, lower back, obliques, adductors, abductors, with serratus, rotator cuff, neck and tibialis as before) are tracked only unless the lifter types a target, which then counts everywhere a target counts; (4) 339 (o), decided against a change after reading the code: a blank RIR read as 0 for estimates was proposed to V and withdrawn before building, since it makes the opposite false PR more common (100 x 8 blank reads as to failure, the same 100 x 8 rated 2 the week after is then a new best and a PR, for identical work, from tapping RIR; the reason r20 chose the lift's usual RIR, `rirUsualIn`); the usual reading stays for every estimate, its one weak spot (a blank set done much harder than usual reads a few percent high) said in EVIDENCE.md; (6) Errors kept with their reasons, where the code had dropped them (V, 2026-10-25): each address three.js fails to load from is in the error log with its own error (section 10; a file that would not fetch reads the same whether missing or refused, as an import is not told the reply's status); a sync request that times out and then fails keeps that failure (`withTimeout`, section 11: a full copy read only as no answer); a refused sign-in (section 12), Copy my log in's unreadable month (section 13), Undo's store and steps, and another tab's save read back; the rest of the catches that log nothing were read one by one and are expected (blocked storage, focus, scroll and the like). (7) The checks made stronger: the screen guards of r57, r58 and r59 read the spaces a browser draws between elements (`screenOf`'s spaces), so a space lost between a date and a tag shows (r58 compared text without spaces, which could not); r57 to r58 and r56.2 to r57 were rechecked so and differ only by r58's tip button; a cycle's days ahead against dates worked out by hand (section 14), not the app's projection alone; `tests/r25.js` takes only the calendar's sheet name, its pull-down check reads that name (it read 'cal', which could no longer fail), and its months check says what 329 changed. (8) Found after (V, 2026-10-25: "cover bases"): Today's fields keep what is typed through a draw since this release, so a weigh-in or tape measurement typed in pounds or inches stayed in place when the unit changed under it (a sync from another device) and was saved as kilograms or centimetres (180 lb as 180 kg); those fields are made again, empty, when the unit changes (section 15, which fails on eb28bd8 and passes on r58, which drew them fresh). A workout started from a day ahead is saved on today, with that day's plan (checked). The engine suite reached its 15 minutes in a full run here: it ran its batches on one core of two (838 s alone), and runs them on every core now (548 s alone), and alone in a full run, as each GitHub shard runs it (`tests/run.js`); the engine itself was not at fault. (9) The second review (2026-10-25, on 93b20b2, over eb28bd8 to 93b20b2, the strongest model; each reproduction rerun on 93b20b2, 2f6a50d and r58 before acting): one finding, high, introduced by eb28bd8 and fixed with a case that fails on it (section 16): Body's weigh-in date was kept as an attribute, which a date field once set (by the lifter, or by a save putting today back) no longer follows, so the morning after an evening weigh-in, after any draw, it still said yesterday and Log put the morning's weight over the evening's, with no Undo; the field is now made again each day (keyed by the date), keeping a date chosen within the day through a draw (section 7). From its suspicions, built: a sign-in that cannot start is logged since this release (section 12) and is tried each time the app comes back to the screen, so offline it could fill the error log and push an earlier crash out of the 60 kept and the 10 a problem report carries; the same error straight after itself is now one line with its count (`n`, shown "(N times)" in Settings; section 17, which fails on 93b20b2 and r58). Left, as limits of the check only: `screenOf`'s spaces reading does not read a space drawn beside a hidden inline element between two runs of text, and reads one after a line break where none is drawn; a loss there would not show, as none at all showed before r59. (10) V asked whether this was all certain (2026-10-25); it was not: the second review's note that a weigh-in typed with no draw before it still took yesterday's date held on every build, r58 too, since nothing draws the app again when it comes back to the screen without an account: opened the next morning and typed into at once, Body's date said yesterday and Log put the morning's weight over the evening's. The app brought back to the screen on a new day now draws again (`drawnBeforeToday` in view.ts; section 18, which fails on r58 and every r59 commit before it); brought back the same day it does not, so nothing being typed is touched. (11) The third review (2026-10-25, on 531b458, over 93b20b2 to 531b458; each reproduction rerun on 531b458 and r58 before acting): the new-day draw waited out a sheet left open overnight, and closing it without an action (Escape, Back, a tap outside, ✕) drew nothing, so Today stayed yesterday's and a weigh-in typed at once went over the evening's (on r58 too); closing a sheet now draws the new day (section 19). From the r59 review: (a) built in r59 (V, 2026-10-25: "do what is sensible"; it shows on the calendar only and changes no history, stat, target, trend or plan): a cycle's days ahead walked on through a rest week marked ahead, so the week after it was labelled with days the cycle would not be on (the next session starts where the cycle stood); the cycle is now held through the rest week (`cycleProjection`'s restWeeks, the calendar's days ahead; `tests/r59.js` section 14, which fails on eb28bd8; the Guide says so). The finger now rests 150 ms at the drop (20 of 20 under load, r59 and r58). `tests/r29.js` (two drags lifted 30 ms after the last move) and `tests/r57.js` section 3 (50 ms after a redraw) race the same 50 ms; neither has failed, so they are left as they are and recorded here. The independent review of the r59 and r60 stack (#55, #56; 2026-10-25, Opus 5.5, briefed to report correctness and data loss only, each finding with a reproduction run on the stack and on the build before it; f4e3a8d included). Each reproduction was rerun before acting (Chromium), and each was confirmed: (1) medium: a tick that waited for its weight (344) was made when the weight was typed even after the reps were cleared, and Finish saved 135 x 0, a missed set the lifter never logged; the waiting tick now needs the set's reps (or a 0 typed), else it is dropped; (2) medium: two tabs on one workout, a load typed in one while the other taps a checkmark, lost the tick from the save and the load from the first tab, since r60 saved twice for one tap (the grey reps, then the tick); each tap is now one change saved once (`act` running the tick inside the tap's change), and the older weakness under it, on r59 too for an untick, is closed: a tab no longer saves over another's save it has not yet heard (`saveNow` merges it first, `onStorage` passes by a write since replaced, every write of the log notes itself as the last saved); (3) low: a field remade by a redraw and given its focus back took the next digit as a new number (13 then 5 gave 5): a load field is armed to be typed over only by a tap or by Next, never by a focus given back; (4) low: Next on a set waiting for its weight moved the focus on, and the weight typed went to the next set; it now stays on the waiting set's load; (5) low (f4e3a8d only): after a tap, a caret moved by an arrow key or End was overruled by the first key; those keys now end the replacing. Each fix has a check in `tests/r60.js` section 8 that fails on f4e3a8d (four of them on r59 too). Not checked by the review: WebKit or an iPhone, mobile keyboards with `execCommand`, a real account or two phones.

(A) The move: Today with a workout open moves whole into typed lit-html modules: `workout.ts` (layer 4: the session screen as `viewLogger` drew it, its taps and fields, auto-mark and the grey reps, the effort strip, the folds and the reorder after a first tick, Finish and Discard, the exercise drag) and the next-set adjustment of `src/17-adjust.js` as a function of the model in layer 2 (`nextSetsIn`, engine, so the seeded suite of (C) runs it in Node), applied to the session in `workout.ts`. Every change to the session in progress goes through `act`, which gains one option: a change to the session in progress alone leaves the caches (nothing they hold reads it; the legacy typing path skipped `invalidate` for that reason, and doing it on every keystroke would rebuild the index each time); Finish, which writes sessions, cardio and settings, drops them as now. The legacy in-place patches of the screen (`afterSet`, `setDone`'s classes, `updateSessBar`, `updateProgress`, `refreshPR`, `updatePlates`, `refreshGrey`, `refreshGreyW`, `carryLoad`'s fields, `rirShowStrip`'s inserted strip) go: the screen is drawn again and lit-html changes only what differs, the effort strip a part of the template (`ui.rirAt`). What the screen opens stays with the sheet code by its data-act until the sheets move (the exercise menu, the picker, Short on time, the calibration set, the heaviest load, the load step, the note, Type or dictate sets, a lift's history and muscles, Pick for me, the recap), the menu's own moves and removal (`bUp`, `bDown`, `bDel`, `wAdd`, `bSs`) becoming the module's; the recap opens through `fx`. Held by `tests/r60.js` section 1: the screen element for element and in visible text against r59 in at least 30 sessions and settings (a plan day, blank, past, an edit, deload, lighter, made lighter, superset, folded and opened, adjusted up, down and by reps, calibration due, the check-in, the bodyweight ask, cardio, kg and lb, RIR off, RPE and halves, timed, per side, assisted, bodyweight with no load, plates, a PR row, a missed set, a lift twice, an injury, warm-ups and drops, Fill in suggested loads, the effort strip open), and every existing suite unchanged. (B) 345 item 11: a field bound to the log keeps text typed and not yet confirmed through a draw; a confirmed value the log saved differently still replaces it (r57's review case). (C) 341 (b), V's five rules, closing 339 (p), in `nextSetsIn` and the return after a break: the seeded-history suite across lifts, kinds, units, racks and breaks holds that no return asks past the pre-break working load, every adjusted load leaves its stated reps at 2 or more in reserve by the same estimate, every load is on the rack or plates, an up is never lighter than its set, and a return done as asked never asks less next time; V's worked examples as cases (165 x 10 at 3 gives 185 x 6; 165 x 6 at 3 gives 170; 165 x 8 x 3 unrated gives 170 next session); EVIDENCE.md gets 3 to 5 as conventions. (D) 341 (a): a tap on a load field, grey or filled, selects its number; a grey one left untouched stays grey and follows the adjustment; one tap to log unchanged; to be tried on V's iPhone (iOS selection). (E) 344: on a lift that takes a load (not bodyweight, not a timed hold), a tick or auto-mark with no load typed and none suggested does not tick: the load field is focused and marked, "Add the weight first"; Finish's question stays the net. (F) 343 (7), 339 (g), 338 (B): "New machine or gym" in an exercise's menu marks the lift's exercise in this session (`nw`, written last, absent unless set, so older records keep their hash and sync); a marked session starts a new stretch for the lift as a break does (readings, targets, trend, stalls, PR frame and dumbbell spacing read from it), its history kept and shown; the block re-reads its suggestion as a first session at the new machine; tapped again before Finish it is taken off; a normalize default, migration and way-back cases, and the sync's key order held. with 336, the menu says the phone is not saving first); (5) a log kept aside came back joined with demo data and the starter routine (it comes back as it was when nothing here is the lifter's; otherwise only what is theirs joins); (6) Add it with two workouts in progress opened the other and closed this phone's, unsaid (this phone's stays open, the other's ticked sets and cardio are saved in History, and the phone says so); (7) cardio edited on the phone whose copy lost was dropped and (8) cardio deleted on the phone that finished came back (cardio merges three ways against the agreed copy); (9) the next sets' adjusted load moved to the wrong row after a reload or a merge (the adjustment carries its row's id); (10) a join put the other phone's warm-up last (it keeps the saved session's order); (11) a join of cardio alone said sets were added (it says cardio). Not reproduced or not checked by that review: WebKit (GitHub runs every suite there), IndexedDB slow or refused during the account switch, a browser without Web Locks, real network latency, and Delete my account with a log kept aside (reasoned: the log left on the phone joins the next account signed in to as a first link, as after any forgotten notes). 336 is fixed with it, being the same data loss. A watch or another device writing the same log later needs what r56.1 built (two copies of one workout merged set by set), so nothing is to be built for it now.

Offline, saved on this device" with nothing saved, on r56.1 and on `baselines/r55.html` alike.

Your log stays on this phone.", signs out and forgets its sync notes; an expired sign-in is never read so (a guard); the stand-in server now keeps a deleted account's access token valid until it expires, as a real token is; (4) 327 a: Type or dictate sets ticks the sets in range and leaves a set far off for the tick's question, checked in order as if the sets before it were ticked (a light 0 cannot reach it: the parser refuses 0 reps); (5) 327 b: a load within half the last digit the screens show (0.05) of an earlier load typed in the other unit is that load, for PRs and for a target beaten by load (`prSameLoad`, `blockProgress`); (6) 327 c: a bar lift on the full bar typed as the total, with no step of its own, in a gym whose plates are all multiples of the smallest, is loaded at the bar plus pairs of plates: its next load, lighter days, deloads, steps, adjustments and warm-ups land on those loads, a repeat at a load now unloadable going to the one under it and a step up to the next one above last time, in place of the rounding onto the unit's step for such lifts (`plateGridIn`, `plateSnap`, `moveIn`); with Your gym not set, as before (a guard); (7) 327 d: a lift trained in two rep ranges restarts each on its own first session back after a break, a range trained before a break staying its own after it however long the break (`rangeInUse`, read by the target and by `backDaysIn`); (8) 327 e: Undo merges the session in progress from its three versions all normalized, so Undo after Make it lighter puts the exercises back, at once, after a set logged since, and after a reload; (9) 328 d: Load demo data while signed in is not done, and says demo data is for looking around before signing in: loaded signed in it synced into the account, and signing out to load it would make the next sign-in a first link, which brings back what other phones deleted meanwhile. (c) Your gym's plates are not kept to on a barbell lift last logged off the new plate spacing: bench at 230 lb, the 2.5 lb plates turned off (45 + 10n loadable): next 240, 230 or 200, a lighter day 200 or 180, warm-ups 90, 140, 130, 80 and 120, while the plate line itself says 2.5 lb not loadable; in kg without 1.25 kg plates after 102.5: next 107.5, 102.5 or 92.5, a lighter day or deload 87.5 or 82.5; a kg log read in lb with no 2.5 lb plates: bench 220, squat 310, lighter days 190 and 260; dumbbells snap to the rack (`rackSnap`), plates do not; ## V's open decisions, placed (V, 2026-10-19: "track and implement them where most appropriate, safest, optimal, impactful")
Each in a release of its own size, small and contained first, so a problem in one cannot ride in with another. Each change gets a test that fails before it, and the release gets the end-to-end audit, the lifter suite (`tests/lifter.js`) and one independent review.
V, 2026-10-20 (r55 frozen on that rule): a release ships once the independent review of its own code is clean; only a problem found in its own new code is fixed in it, and a finding the release before already had goes to the next release, so a release closes instead of growing. The order is r55.1, r56, r57, r58, r59, with nothing added between them but breakage or data loss; the larger exercise library comes after r59.
- r55.1 (small, contained, right after r55): built (330): (a) 325 a, a 0 ticked at a light load (well under the lift's recent working load) asks Log it as missed or Fix it, since a slip of the finger is likelier there than a missed attempt; a 0 at or near a working load stays logged as missed at once (V's answer of 2026-10-19); (b) imports keep Strong's workout notes and durations and Hevy's durations and supersets; (c) a phone still signed in to an account deleted elsewhere says the account is gone and that its log stays on the phone, instead of saying it will sync; (d) the end-to-end audit of r55's findings that r54 already had (item 327); (e) the review's older findings 328 (b), as V decided it on 2026-10-20, and 328 (d).
- r56 (storage and sync, phase 4): the move alone (331), nothing a lifter sees changed. r56.1 (332), on the typed code: 328 b, a phone holding another account's log asks before merging (V's call of 2026-10-20, moved here by V the same day; the spec review's notes are in 330); one workout open on two phones at once keeps both phones' sets (each phone remembers the session in progress it last agreed with the account, so the two are merged set by set), with the end-to-end audit's reproduction (327) and the review's (328 a and c) as its first tests; 305, 307 and 308 from the move's reviews; nothing else a lifter sees changes. Both built; r56 waits on V's merge, r56.1 on r56.
- r57 to r60: phase 5, the screens, as planned (ARCHITECTURE.md), with 329 (looking ahead in the week bar and the calendar) when they move.
- After r59 (its own release): 325 b, a larger exercise library, each lift with its muscles, pattern, equipment and load meaning set as the library's are and checked against the analytics (volume, trends, swaps).

Also from r51 (the outside review's config values): the bundle keeps every module declaration (treeShaking off, so the move checks see every module on the page), so config's library addresses, versions, fonts and password-check origin, which only the build and the markup read, ride in the app script as values nothing on the page reads (about 25 lines; harmless, never run as code beyond their own definition). The clean fix is the build reading those from config while the page bundle leaves them out, which needs the move check's rule that every module declaration is on the page to know which ones the page does not use. 302. V's call (from the r48 check): the ready-made routines rest 45 or 60 s on most isolation lifts (99 items, 12 at 45 s and 87 at 60 s; calves, laterals, curls, pressdowns, face pulls, abs). Singer 2024 found a small benefit to growth from resting more than 60 s and nothing past 90 s. Raising those to 75 or 90 s is a number change, so it waits for V. What it touches, said now: a template's signature changes with it, so an untouched copy of a ready-made routine on a phone would read as the lifter's own (`plainTemplate`, `starterSigs`) until the signatures of the old copies are listed beside the new; the time estimates of those routines go up a few minutes; nothing logged changes. Decided by V, 2026-10-25: 90 s ("go with 90 then but easy to change"), built in r62 with the library. As one named value with its source beside it (Singer 2024: a small benefit to growth past 60 s, no difference detected past 90 s; the volume lost to short rest levels off there), which every isolation item now resting under 90 s in the template table reads, so a change is one edit; items already at 90 s or more keep their own. Routines already on a phone keep what they have (a migration never overwrites a value in the lifter's log; each exercise's rest is the lifter's to change in Plan); the old templates' signatures stay listed so an untouched copy still reads as ready-made (`plainTemplate`, `starterSigs`), and `tests/` hold both. Measured 2026-10-25: 78 isolation items rest under 75 s across eight routines; at 90 s a training day gains about 0.7 to 4.1 minutes (at 75 s, 0.3 to 1.8). Placed in r64 by the r63 audit's plan (2026-10-27): it is decided and does not need the library (tracker row 302).

290. The three structural risks the audit named, for the record: one 10,700-line script with hand-escaped templates and shared state (the security and fuzz suites are the wall); sync depending on `normalize` staying hash-stable across builds (nothing mechanical enforces it beyond the migration suite); the dashboard steps (SQL, functions, email templates, branch protection) living only in OWNER.md and applied by hand. Not fixes; what to weigh before growing the app or its users. r45 adds a mechanical check for the second: tests/seams.js (random saves normalize the same twice, reassemble from their sync documents, and come back unchanged through the previous build), and tests/hygiene.js checks the previous build is kept as a baseline and loaded by the migration suite. The first and third stay as they are, on purpose: see the r45 note to V. 2026-10-16: the first risk is now being retired by item 303 (typed modules, ARCHITECTURE.md); retired in r63, when the legacy script was deleted. The second is 339 (n), deferred by V on 2026-10-27.

366. Two-a-days, the rules (V, 2026-10-30: tracked and tagged to a release, carried in a larger one, not a release of its own; placed in r66 with the library, 325 b). What works today (r34, item 226): a second session on one day, from Today or as a past workout; a day with two asks which; both count for the week, volume, PRs, trends and recovery. Three gaps: (a) a second session's target steps up from the first: with bench 140 x 10 x 3 at 8:00, the target at 17:00 is "add 5 lb", 145 (tracker NEW-18, reproduced on r65). V: not the last session's numbers blindly; a logical, practical process and never absurd advice; overload within one day is rare; maybe the best of the two, or the earlier one, or left to the lifter shown from history (grey, or a number by the setting); do not overcomplicate. (b) The day after a two-a-day: which of the day's sessions sets the next target (V: not simply the most recent). (c) A routine on fixed weekdays has exactly one day a weekday, so it cannot plan a morning and an evening workout; a numbered cycle can, as two days in a row. V: fix it. Proposal for (a) and (b), one rule, to confirm with V before building: a lift's sessions on one day are read together; a second session the same day asks what that day's earlier session did, no step up, the lifter free to push; the next day's target comes from that day's best full session. For (c): a weekday can hold a second workout in a fixed-weekdays routine. Built in r68: (a), (b) and (c) (tracker rows NEW-18 and 366).

192. Owner decision before going beyond friends: a short privacy policy page (what is stored, the email's use, who can see it, how to delete). California's CalOPPA expects one from an online service that collects emails from California residents; the in-app lines cover the substance but are not a policy page. A lawyer's look is item 189. Added from the 2026-10-16 research check (r48), each for a lawyer to confirm, none built: HIPAA almost certainly does not apply (no provider or insurer); the FTC Health Breach Notification Rule, amended July 2024 to cover fitness apps, likely does once there are public users (a breach plan, and notice within 60 days of year end for under 500 people); Washington's My Health My Data Act has no size threshold and asks for a separate consumer health data policy, opt-in consent, and separate consent before any sharing (Strava or Apple Health exports); California's CCPA thresholds are unlikely to be met at this size; offer the official PAR-Q+ by link rather than a rewrite; no quantified or guaranteed gain claims anywhere (none exist today, tests/r34b.js and r41 keep it so).
189. r31.1 (V): a quiet notice that suggestions are estimates from the log, not medical or coaching advice, with the full text behind an i (Settings > Your data under the privacy line, the first-run Set up page, the Guide's targets topic). This is a sensible baseline, not legal advice. Owner decision for later: if the app is offered to people beyond friends, have a lawyer review a short Terms of Use and privacy notice (and whether to require ticking it on first run).

## Cloud sync and accounts
66. Every device should run the same build. While a phone still runs r21 or older, a weigh-in deleted or corrected on that phone is not removed on r22 phones (r22 only adds what an older build writes), and the older phone shows no body data. Nothing is lost. Tell friends to tap Reload when "New version ready" shows. r23 adds one more reason: assisted pull-ups and dips store the load as bodyweight minus help (a negative number), and a phone still on r22 reads those sets as bodyweight only (0) and writes them back that way. Update every device before logging an assisted set.
13. An emailed sign-in link without a password was left out: on an iPhone it opens in Safari, whose storage is separate from the home-screen app. A 6-digit emailed code would work instead, if wanted (needs Part 2's email sender).
15. Client-side encryption, optional: the owner cannot read data, but a forgotten passphrase loses the data. V chose to launch without it (2026-09-27). Decide before friends sign up (OWNER.md Now, step 6): until then the owner can read every account's log in Supabase, and the privacy line says so.
21. The standalone app has no Claude features (debrief, Ask Claude, AI swap, Claude reading typed sets); since r29.5 the build leaves their code out (146). With the artifact retired (2026-09-27) they run nowhere. Their code is gone from the source (r62, 304; checked 2026-10-30). Left to decide, later: whether to add another AI service (its key would have to live in a Supabase function, never in the app).

## Asked for on 2026-09-27, for later
48. Custom routine generator (the unmet need: most people will not build or import a routine). From a few answers (goal, days a week, minutes per session, equipment, experience, muscles to bring up) build a routine from the library that meets the weekly targets and stays under the per-session flag, then keep adjusting it from the log. First step already taken in r20: six ready-made routines on the first-run card and in Plan. Demand cannot be measured inside the app, because the privacy line promises no tracking; ask friends who try it instead.
49. Age and sex as optional profile fields, only when a feature needs them (for example strength standards, bodyweight-share estimates, or the female muscle map, item 141). r20 adds the optional name only.
50. Fitness app and health integrations. Web APIs with sign-in (Strava, Fitbit, Garmin, Oura, Whoop, Withings) work from the installed web app, with each service's tokens kept in a Supabase function, never in the app. Apple Health and Android Health Connect have no web API: they need the native wrapper (item 46). CSV export already exists.
51. Payments, if Ironlog is ever charged for (information only, nothing built): Stripe Checkout for a subscription or a one-time lifetime price, the Stripe customer portal for cancelling and card changes, and a webhook into a Supabase function that stores who has paid; the app only reads that flag. Needs terms, a privacy policy, a refund policy and sales tax handling (Stripe Tax). If it ever ships in the App Store, Apple's in-app purchase rules apply to digital features.
67. Import a photo of a handwritten log. Reading handwriting needs an AI service (see 21). Works today without it: on an iPhone, open the photo, press on the text (Live Text), Copy, and paste into Import workouts; the same for a photo of a whiteboard or a printed sheet.
68. Other apps' exports. Strong, Hevy, FitNotes-style columns ("Weight (kgs)") and any sheet with a header row are tested; JEFIT, Fitbod and others are read through the column picker but not tested against a real export. Add one when a friend sends a file.

## Training (a conversation, not an app fix)
39. Program review: Chest 17, Quads 15 and Upper back 14 sets in single sessions; Arms and Shoulders days around 80 minutes.

## Optional features
40. Tape measurement error from your own repeat measurements (TEM, 2.77 x TEM threshold).
42. RIR calibration split by rep band once there is enough data.
46. Native app wrapper (Capacitor), only if needed: rest timer alerts through a locked screen, Apple Health, a real App Store install. Costs $99 a year for Apple and needs a Mac.
229. Later (V asked, not buildable as a web app): widgets for iPhone, Apple Watch, and Android. Apple allows no widgets for web apps, and as far as known Android Chrome neither (only Windows). Needs a native wrapper: Apple's developer program ($99 a year) and a Mac for iPhone and Watch (with item 46 and 169), Google Play ($25 once) for Android.


## Done

Each release's finished items, from r54 back, are in `docs/ARCHIVE.md`.
